Its head lists them, the one shown selected, and `[` `]` pick another: **Server** (`logs/server.log`; with no file, that a server run in the foreground, as the systemd service runs it, logs to its terminal or the journal), **Service** (`logs/service.log`, launchd's output, when it exists), **Console** (`logs/console.log`, below) and each mod's (`logs/mods/<id>.log`, `mod <id>`). Under it the log's size, `following` or `paused (f follows)`, the filter and the search, then its path (its start cut with `…` when it doesn't fit), and a line for a note. The log fills the rest, its long lines wrapped, as plain text (`textsafe.Clean`: no escape reaches the terminal). It opens on the log's end, the last 64 KB read in one chunk, and follows it: each second (the dashboard's tick) it reads what was added after the end, whole lines only; a file now shorter than that end was rotated, and the tab starts it anew, saying so. `↑` `↓` (`k` `j`), `pgup` `pgdn` (`space`) and the wheel scroll, which pauses it; scrolling up past what it holds reads the 64 KB before, one chunk at a time, never the whole file; `g` (`home`) reads back to the start (at most 20000 lines held; following drops the oldest, which scrolling up reads again); `G` (`end`) or `f` follows again. `/` opens a search line (`enter` finds the nearest match up from the end, `esc` drops it); the matches are marked, `n` goes to the next older one, reading older chunks one at a time when what it holds has none, and `N` to the next newer. A line carries a level when it has `level=…`, `[ERROR]`, a word `ERROR`, `WARN`, `INFO` or `DEBUG`, or `error:`, `warning:`, `panic:` or `fatal:`; errors show red, warnings yellow, debug faint, and `v` steps the filter through all, warnings and errors, and errors only (a log whose lines carry none says so). `o` opens the file in `$PAGER` (else `less +G`; `more` on Windows) on this console's terminal when it is on this host, and `c` copies its path (OSC 52). `r` lists the logs again. The action row: `[ ] log · ↑ ↓ pgup pgdn scroll · f follow · / search · n N next · v level · o pager · c copy path · ← → tabs · esc close`. The tab is the human's: `log.list` and `log.read` are human-only, and tm inside an agent refuses them before asking. **The console's log**, `logs/console.log` (`internal/tui/consolelog.go`), is the client side's errors, which the server's log never sees: what the footer showed as an error, a view call that failed, a digest mismatch, a takeover that couldn't be told, a panic with its stack. Every console of the home appends to it, each line `pid N: <time> error: …`; it is rotated at 1 MB (`console.log.1` kept). It holds no keys, pane output or prompts: the attach client's diagnostics, keys among them, go to `TERMINATR_ATTACH_LOG` alone, off unless set.
- **No files in the UI.** No screen, popup, hint or message names the settings file, TOML, a setting's key, a path under `~/.terminatr` or an editor; settings have plain labels. A broken settings file is reported by line (`the settings can't be read: line 3 is broken`). This document and `tm context` (for agents) name the file.
- **Projects sidebar.** A column on the left of every screen, the dashboard, an attached session, `tm attach` and `tm project open` alike, holds the project tree. The right-hand side of every row is a fixed grid, the same on every row so its columns line up down the tree: a count or percent (5 cells), then six glyph columns of two cells each, blank where they don't apply. A Unicode glyph sits in a column's left cell; a Nerd Font icon, which draws two cells wide, fills both, so nothing after it shifts. The 17 cells are:

  | Column | Project row | Coordinator row | Thread row |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | count / % | its open threads (faint at 0; 0 while inactive) | its context use (§8.6), yellow from `[ui] context_hint`, red from 80% | its progress |
  | PR / CI | | | its pull request's checks: `☑` pass, `☒` fail, `☐` running; blank without a PR or checks |
  | state | its coordinator's state, only while the project is folded | its state (`·` when none runs) | its state (`✓` once done) |
  | remote | | `⌁` its remote control is on (§11.2) | |
  | paused | `∥` the user paused it (§11.2) | | |
  | inactive | `⊘` it is inactive (§5.1) | `⊘` dormant | `⊘` dormant |
  | needs you | `⚑` something under it needs you | `⚑` it has questions waiting | `⚑` it waits on you: blocked, in review, or a `Waiting for you` self-report |

  The grid is an ordered column list, the view's (`view.Columns`, `columns` in the view's JSON; none for the order above, so every console agrees). Its ids are part of the mod API and never renamed: `count`, `ci`, `state`, `remote`, `paused`, `inactive`, `needs`, and a mod's own column, `<mod>.<column>` (`review-age.age`, each part `[a-z0-9-]`), in the slot the order gives it. A mod column is as wide as its mod declares (at most 12 cells) and blank until the mod publishes its cells, or while its mod host is down; the columns around it never move for it. One no loaded mod declares takes no room, and an id this tm doesn't know is left out. `ci` is the PR/CI column, the built-in `pr-ci-column` mod's (its column `pr-ci-column.ci`; an order naming that means the same, and it shows once): it has no other drawing, so while the mod is off no mod declares it and it takes no room. An enabled code mod's column whose `where` names a sidebar slot and which the order doesn't name goes before the state column, in mod order, so it shows without a layout. Its cells are the mod's (§5.3 **Cells**): a project row's for its project (`sidebar.project`), a coordinator row's for its session (`sidebar.coordinator`), a thread row's for the thread, else its task, else its session (`sidebar.thread`); cut to the column less one blank, and blank on rows of slots the column doesn't name. The safety columns (state, paused, inactive, needs you) always show: an order that leaves one out gets it back at its end. At most 32 columns. Nothing sets the order yet: a layout mod will (the mods design, Phase 1).

  A long name or title is cut with `…` before the grid gives way. Archived projects are left out of the sidebar and the dashboard (§5.1):

  ```
   PROJECTS                      2   │   the header, with the count of projects; a click: every project's NEEDS YOU
   ■ terminatr        2           ⚑  │   a project: its folder mark, name, open threads; ⚑ something under it needs you
   └─ coordinator   40%   ● ⌁        │   its coordinator: context use, state (· when none runs); ⌁ its remote control is on
      ├─ T3 Boots…  60% ☑ ●          │   under it, each open thread: its task's id, title, progress, PR checks, state
      └─ t-0004 C…        ▲       ⚑  │   a thread without a task (ad hoc, adopted): its own id; └─ the last row
   ■ foodperfect      0              │
   └─ coordinator         ○          │
  ```

  - **Active first, then the inactive divider; drawn as a folder tree.** The active projects come first, the **pinned** ones at the top (`p` on a project's row, or `pin to the top` in its menu; `p` again, `unpin`, puts it back): pinning changes the order only, with no glyph, and is the project's own `pinned` setting (§11.2). Below them a faint divider row, `── ▸ inactive (3) ──` (`▾` while unfolded; ` ─▸3──` in the slim strip), holds the **inactive** projects (§5.1), folded by default. The divider and the inactive projects under it are **pinned to the bottom of the tree**, just above the rule and the details line (and above a band of sections below the tree): the free rows go between the last project (or, with paired servers, the last group) and the divider; with no spare row the divider follows what is above it, and the tree scrolls as ever. The cursor goes through the rows top to bottom as drawn. Its keys: `→` unfolds it, `←` folds it, and `enter` or a click toggles it (`fold` / `unfold` in its menu); `space` does nothing on it. An active project starts unfolded: `←` on its row (`fold` in its menu) folds it to its row alone, which then shows its coordinator's state in the state column and whatever under it needs you; `→` (`unfold`) unfolds it. `z` folds every active project and the divider (collapsing the inactive projects), `Z` unfolds them all, the inactive projects expanded; the cursor moves up to what still shows. Folding never activates or deactivates. What is folded is the view's (`view.Tree`, `view.tree`: the active projects folded, the inactive ones expanded, the divider open), and `ui.json` keeps the last tree set as the tree new views start with (§5.1). An inactive project under the open divider is collapsed: its project row alone, its name faint and `⊘` at the far right, so parked projects take a row each. Its count still counts its open threads. The user can expand it like a folder without activating it: `→` on its row, or `expand` in its menu (`←` on its row, or `collapse`, folds it again; `←` on it folded goes up to the divider). Expanded, it shows its coordinator and its open threads **dormant**: their labels faint, `·` as their state glyph and `⊘` in the inactive column, the coordinator's row reading `coordinator` whether a dormant session waits to resume or none was ever started. A dormant thread's row doesn't attach (`t-0001 is dormant: start demo's coordinator, or activate demo`). **Starting the coordinator from its row** (`enter` or a click on it, `start the coordinator` in its menu) activates the project, as `tm project activate` does, with no question: its dormant coordinator and threads resume (a coordinator that had none starts fresh) and the coordinator attaches. Expanding alone never activates. Activating expands it for good (it is active), and deactivating collapses it, also in every view that had it expanded. A project row has a folder mark, its name (bold; the current project's mark and name in the accent colour) and its grid (above): its needs-you column marks one of its threads blocked or waiting on someone, one of its tasks that needs the user (`review` or `blocked`, the tasks NEEDS YOU lists) or its coordinator's questions. Under it, on tree connectors (`├─`, and `└─` for the last row, as `tree` draws them), comes its coordinator, and one level under the coordinator, since threads are its, each open thread with its name and title, each with its grid. A thread's name is its task's id (`T3 Bootstrap re…`), since the user and the coordinator talk in task ids; a thread without a task (ad hoc, adopted) keeps its thread id (`t-0004 Claude s…`). The thread id is behind-the-scenes: it shows in the details (the dashboard's details panel, the info panel, `tm thread show`, `tm session list`). The dashboard's thread rows lead the same way, with the thread id in brackets after the state (`T3 Bootstrap repo + spec  ● working  (t-0002)  60% …`), and so does the status bar (`terminatr › T3 Bootstrap repo  60% …`) and the session details. A thread whose agent has a question menu open (§3.3, **Ask**) says `question open` as its reason, and the dashboard's details panel (and a session's) list the question and its options by number, as `tm thread show` does. A long name or title is cut with `…`, a title right after its last letter. A thread's name is never cut: the title gives way, down to none of it; in a sidebar too narrow for the grid and the name, mods' columns give way first (the rightmost first; the PR/CI column is one), then the count/% column, and narrower still the name is left out. At the default width a thread row has room for its name and a few cells of title (`T232 Sid…`). Every row of the full sidebar leaves one blank column before the border so the glyphs don't touch it. The connectors are faint. Resolved threads disappear. (The coordinator is a project's only child, so no `│` continuation line is needed.)
  - **Icons.** A global setting (`,`, *Icons*: `auto`, `nerd`, `unicode` or `ascii`; `[ui] icons` in `config.toml`, `auto` when unset) picks the glyphs of the tree and of the states, todos and progress bars wherever `tm` draws them. `unicode` is box drawing and standard shapes, nothing from the Private Use Area (`■`, `├─`, `●○▲◌◆✓·`, `▰▱`). `nerd` uses Nerd Font icons: a folder (open for the current project), a robot before the coordinator, a git branch before each thread, Font Awesome state glyphs, and a short connector (`├╴`) to make room. `ascii` is plain ASCII for any font (`+`, `` |- `` and `` `- ``, `*` working, `!` blocked, `o` idle, `?` the hint, `##---` progress). `auto` is decided by each console for its own terminal, since the font is the terminal's: Nerd Font icons when `TERM_PROGRAM` is `ghostty` (Ghostty bundles the Nerd Font symbols), Unicode elsewhere. No set uses emoji, whose width differs between terminals; every glyph is one cell (a connector two), which `TestIconWidths` checks for each set. A Nerd Font icon draws over the blank after it, so the sidebar gives every glyph a column two cells wide, and the nerd set puts a blank after the folder, coordinator and thread icons. The PR checks are `☑ ☒ ☐` in `unicode`, Font Awesome's check, times and notch circles in `nerd`, `+ x ~` in `ascii`. An icon set can also be written in TOML (`parseIcons`, the data format of an icons mod, §5.3, whose id the setting then names): `name`, `base` (`unicode`, `nerd` or `ascii`; `unicode` when unset), `wide`, and any glyph field of the built-in sets under the same names (`working`, `ciPass`, `barOn`, …). A field the file leaves out takes the base set's glyph, and loading enforces what `TestIconWidths` enforces: one cell per glyph (two for `mid` and `end`), no emoji, no Private Use Area glyph unless the base is `nerd`, only ASCII when the base is `ascii`; an unknown key, a wrong type or a bad glyph is an error naming each fault. A change applies at once in the console that made it; others pick it up when they next open.
  - The current project is on the dashboard the one it lists (the view's current project, else the first); while attached, the focused pane's project (else the view's).
  - The row you are on is drawn in reverse video (from the folder mark or the label on, the connectors left plain, through the blank column up to the border, so a Nerd Font icon in the last column that draws wider than its cell, like the bell, shows whole): the current project's row on the dashboard, the focused session's coordinator or thread row while attached. The status bar names the project too (`terminatr › coordinator`), so the context is never lost.
  - A click on a project row shows that project's dashboard (`view.project`); on a coordinator row attaches its coordinator (started if none runs); on a thread row attaches the thread's pane (a thread without a running session says so). This works from the dashboard, under a popup, and while attached: the view changes on every console. In `tm attach` and `tm project open` a click hands the console over to a full one of view `main` (§3.3). The prefix then `]` and `[` open coordinators from the keys.
  - It is 36 columns wide by default (its border included), so a thread row has room for its id and a few words of its title beside the grid, at least 14, with no maximum: only the window bounds it, since the panes keep 60 columns, and a wide sidebar shows long names and titles whole. Dragging its border, `{` and `}` (2 columns) on the dashboard, or the prefix then `{` or `}` while attached, change the width; `b` (prefix then `b` while attached) turns it into the slim strip, then hides it, then shows the full sidebar again (full → slim → hidden → full; a layout mod's `sidebar.mode` only sets where it starts). A layout mod (§5.3) may also put it on the **right** (`sidebar.side`) and give its width as a share of the window (`sidebar.width = "25%"`, 10 % to 60 %): the share is kept as a share, so each console makes it columns of its own window (at least 14), then the rules below apply; dragging or `{` `}` make it columns again. On the right its border is its first column, the dashboard and the pane start at the window's first column, and while attached the order is the pane, the info panel, the sidebar, which keeps to the console's own right edge. The width, the side, the share and hidden are the view's (`view.Sidebar`; side, share and hidden since protocol 14), so every console of the view follows; `ui.json` (§5.1) keeps the last one set as the width of new views. A width saved there stays when the default changes; only a `ui.json` without one starts at the default. A width too wide for the window is shown cut to leave the panes 60 columns, and kept: it comes back in a wider window. `{` steps down from the width shown; `}` at the window's limit changes nothing.
  - When a full sidebar would leave less than 60 columns even at its own width or the default, whichever is less, it shrinks to the slim strip, 11 columns listing projects only, under `PROJEC…`: a state column (the coordinator's state, `⊘` while inactive), the slug cut to 5 cells with `…`, and the needs-you column (` ● term…⚑`); the state column says `∥` for a paused project instead of an idle coordinator's state (a blocked or waiting one still shows: it needs you first); the current project's name in the accent colour. A click on one shows its dashboard. A narrow window never takes it away.
  - **Hidden** (`b`, or a layout's `sidebar.mode = "hidden"`): no sidebar at all, 0 columns on both screens; the pane and the dashboard get the whole window, `tab` (prefix then `tab`) skips it, and `b` brings it back. Since its needs-you column is gone, a `⚑ N` badge in the warning colour counts what needs the user in every project of this server (the all-projects NEEDS YOU, **All projects** above, less the paired servers'): on the dashboard at the right of the header, before the server's state, where a click shows the all-projects NEEDS YOU with its first row selected; while attached at the right end of the status bar, where a click opens the dashboard there. `ui.json` writes a hidden sidebar as `"mode": "hidden"` with `"slim": true`, so an older tm shows the strip.
  - **Mods' sections** (`internal/tui/sidesections.go`; **Mod sections**, below). A mod's `sidebar.section` is a section of the **band**, below the tree after a blank row (above it when the layout's `[sidebar] sections` lists it before `"tree"`, §5.3), for the current project, else the mod's tree for all projects; a `sidebar.project.section` is a section under each active, unfolded project, after its last thread (its coordinator when it has none), at thread depth, for that project: none under a folded or an inactive project or the inactive divider, and a project the filter leaves out takes its sections with it. A section's header is a row of the tree (`▾ TICKER ──── terminatr`: the fold mark, the title, in capitals in the band and as the mod wrote it under a project, the badge at the right end of the rule, or else the band's project in faint), its body the mod's lines, indented, which are no rows: `↑` `↓` skip them, and `w` `W`, `1`–`9` and the filter skip the headers. On a header `→` unfolds and `←` folds the section (on a folded per-project header `←` goes up to its project), `enter` opens the view the mod names (`opens`) or else folds or unfolds it, and `z` `Z` fold and unfold the sections with the projects; `space`, `p` and the preview do nothing there. The details line names the section and its mod (`Ticker · ticker mod (built in)`, the view's `description` under it) and the key bar its keys (`← fold · ⏎ open`). A click on a header or its body gives the sidebar the keyboard, puts the cursor on the header and does what `enter` does; the wheel scrolls the tree with its sections. Band sections ask 4 lines unless they say (at most 12), a per-project one 2 (at most 4). All sections take at most half the sidebar's body (its rows less the header and the footer), the band above the tree at most a quarter, the per-project ones together at most a quarter and the headers alone a quarter; a body under 12 rows shows none. Where they don't fit they give way so that every row with `⚑`, the cursor's row and the row you are on still show, and every tree row keeps its whole grid: per-project bodies first (the bottom project's first), then the band above's, then the band below's, each to its header, then headers, the last one shown saying `+N`; a section cut for room says `…` at its right and comes back when there is room, its fold untouched. While a band section is above the tree, the PROJECTS header (always just above the first project) counts what NEEDS YOU counts as `⚑ N` in the warning colour, as it does whenever a `⚑` row is scrolled off; a click on it goes to the first such row, as `w`. The slim strip and the hidden sidebar have no sections, and their mods hear `view.closed`. A section shows from the mod's first tree for it: until then it takes no row, not even its header. A failed mod's shows its header and one red `▲ mod failed: …` line (a per-project mod's once, in the band below the tree), never a stale tree.
  - **A failing PR host** is core's, whatever the ticker mod does: while the ticker's PR polls fail for a project that has a PR host (`gh_failing`, §7.5), its row shows a red `▲` right after the name (the name is cut before it; the grid is untouched), whose tooltip and details line say `PR host failing: its PR host CLI failed on 3 PR polls in a row · see the inbox`, and the slim strip draws its slug in red. Both screens read it from `ticker.json`, once a poll.
  - **Paired servers** (`internal/tui/sidepeers.go`, §16): with a paired server the tree is in **server groups**: a header row per server, this one first (` ◉ studio · this server`, the host name), its projects under it as above, then each paired server by alias, with a blank line above its header, with the projects it shares from the server's mirror (`peer.list`, §16.10; `Data.Peers`, read on each poll by the dashboard and the attach view alike). With none no header is drawn and the sidebar is as above. This server's inactive divider and projects come after the last group, pinned to the bottom as above, with a blank line above the divider so it isn't taken for part of the last group (only this server has inactive projects: a paired server shares its active ones). A paired server's header says its link: `◉ mini · 38 ms` (linked, the last ping), `◌ mini · connecting`, `○ mini · offline 4m · relay` (how long, and why: `relay`, `peer`, `key refused`, `old tm`; `⊘` for the last two); its glyph is in the link's colour, its name bold, faint while offline. Its projects use the same grid, from the last snapshot: the count of open threads, the coordinator's state when folded, `⚑` when something there needs its user; unfolded, the coordinator (its context use and state) and the threads (task id, title, progress from the task's steps or the session's todos, PR checks, state) under it. A project whose link is down or whose last read failed is **stale**: its row alone, dimmed, its last values kept, `·` for its state; the details line says `last seen 14:02`. Remote rows open nothing on that server: `enter`, a click and the preview on a remote project show its **remote dashboard** (below); a remote **coordinator or thread** opens its **pane** with `enter` or a click when that server shares its panes with this one (§16.12): the view shows the session `mini/s-41`, and its details line says `its pane opens here` (or `its pane: view-only`); otherwise `enter` says why in the status bar (`mini doesn't share panes with this server: on mini, tm peer panes <this server> view, or write`, `mini is offline: its panes open once it is back`, `mini runs tm 0.27.0, which has no remote panes; update it to open them`) and the details line says `panes off` or `view-only`; a remote project's row and its server's count say `view-only` when that server's panes are view-only for this one and `panes writable` when they take keys, nothing when they are off; the preview opens no pane; `space` and `p` do nothing there, `w` `W` skip them and `1`–`9` count this server's projects only. A paired server's header folds and unfolds with `enter`, `←` `→` or a click (`mini · 38 ms · 2 projects` while folded); a remote project with `←` `→` as a project does (`←` on it folded goes up to its server); `z` folds them too and `Z` unfolds them; the folds are the view's tree's (`folded`, as `@mini` and `@mini/acme-api`, which no slug can be). The filter narrows this server's projects only: the groups go while it shows. The slim strip draws each header as its glyph and a rule in the link's colour, no name, and each remote project as a project. The header `PROJECTS N` counts this server's. No key is added: a remote coordinator is reached through a link (§16.11), and the paired servers are in the `,` popup's Peers tab.
  - **Remote dashboard** (`internal/tui/peerdash.go`): a paired server's project, from the mirror (`peer.list`, §16.10), drawn as the coordinator's info panel draws one of this server's (its ProjectWatch: what needs its user, the inbox, the threads, the tasks on deck), without the panel's key and command hints and with nothing to click. The view's current project is then `@mini/acme-api` (as the folds name it, which no slug can be), so every console of the view shows it, and its row is the one you are on. The header's title is the breadcrumb, ` tm dashboard · mini › acme-api`; above the panel, the link and how fresh the copy is (`◉ mini · linked 38 ms · synced 2s ago`; down: `○ mini · offline 4m · relay · last seen 14:02`; `· view-only` or `· panes writable` follows by that server's pane level for this one, nothing when off, the rest dimmed, its last values kept), the project's goal and `paused`, and its coordinator (`coordinator ● working · 84k / 200k context · opus`), then a faint line saying its coordinator is reached through a link (`tm project link`). The peer's own times (a PR last checked) are read on its clock (the mirror's offset). A project no longer shared, or a server no longer paired, says so. Nothing that would act on the project is in the key bar or the `≡` menu, not even greyed: `enter`, `space`, `a`, `t`, `i`, `T`, `z`, `|`, `<` `>` and the mods' commands and views say `acme-api is mini's: nothing here acts on it`; the list's keys and the wheel scroll it; `tab`, `;`, `n`, `]` `[`, the sidebar's keys, `,`, `?` and `prefix+q` work as anywhere (`≡ menu · ↑ ↓ scroll · , settings · ? help · prefix+q quit`). It has no details panel. A remote project's right-click menu has `show its dashboard` and its fold.
  - The pane and the dashboard get the window less the sidebar. A width change is a layout change: the pane the view shows is resized to its new rectangle (§3.3).
  - While a sidebar is shown, the attach view keeps the outer terminal's mouse reporting on (button events and drags; every motion, 1003, while the full sidebar shows, for its tooltip) so the sidebar can be clicked whatever the focused program wants; the program still only gets mouse events it asked for. The dashboard asks for every motion too while the full sidebar shows.
  - **Tooltip.** With the mouse over one of a row's glyphs, the sidebar says what it means (`working`, `PR checks fail`, `needs you: it waits on you`, `context use`), in its details line, the line under it saying `(hover)`, so there is nothing to close; off the glyphs or off the sidebar it goes. The slim strip has none. Selecting text natively in a pane then needs Shift (Option-drag in some macOS terminals, e.g. iTerm2), as on the dashboard. A plain drag over a pane whose program doesn't take the mouse (a shell) selects in tm instead (**Copying text**, below).
  - **From the keyboard.** On the dashboard `tab` / `shift+tab` move the keyboard between the list, the details panel (when it shows) and the sidebar; the area that has it gets its border in the accent colour (the sidebar's border column, the details panel's divider) and the list's selection is dimmed while it is elsewhere. One model on both screens (`internal/tui/focus.go`): the dashboard's areas are the list, the details panel and the sidebar; a session's are the pane, the sidebar and the info panel beside a thread's or a coordinator's pane (**Info panel**, below). In a session the prefix then `tab` moves the keyboard to the next of them that shows (pane, sidebar, info panel, pane), and `tab` in the sidebar or the info panel does the same. The sidebar's keys, from one table (`sideActions`) that also draws the help: `↑` `↓` (`k` `j`) move its cursor through the rows shown and the `PROJECTS` header, which sits among them where it is drawn (under a band above the tree, over the tree; `View.SideSel` `h:projects`), drawn in the cursor's look while the cursor is on it; `enter` or `→` there shows every project's NEEDS YOU (**All projects**), its preview the same, and the footer says `all projects` with `⏎ show all`; `→` (`l`) on a folded project or the inactive divider unfolds it, and elsewhere does what `enter` does; `←` (`h`) on an unfolded project or the divider folds it, on a row under a project moves up to it, and on a folded inactive project up to the divider; when the cursor has rested on a row for 200 ms the view **previews** it (`treeRow.preview`): a project shows its dashboard, a coordinator or a thread its live session, and a row with nothing running (an inactive project's, a coordinator not started, a thread without a session) its project's dashboard; a preview never starts anything, and the keyboard stays in the sidebar (a bare view, `tm attach`, previews nothing); `enter` goes **into** the row: its target shows, as a click's does (a coordinator not running starts, a dormant one activating its project), and the keyboard follows it to the pane or the dashboard's list (on the divider it folds or unfolds); `z` folds everything and `Z` unfolds everything (above); `p` on a project pins it to the top or unpins it; `c` on a running coordinator saves and clears it (`side.save-clear`, `tm clear`, §7.5 **Auto-clear**), the status bar or footer saying what came of it, and its key bar offers `c save and clear` while its context is past `[ui] context_hint` (the count cell's tooltip says so too); `space` on a project row activates an inactive project or deactivates an active one (§5.1), after a `y` in a dialog when its coordinator or threads run (`Deactivate demo? This stops its coordinator, T3 and T5 now; they resume when you activate it.`), and the status bar or footer says what came of it (`activated demo: resumed its coordinator, T3`); `esc` gives the keyboard back to the list (in a session, to the pane), or first clears a filter; `tab` moves it to the next area. `↑` `↓` go round the ends (the sidebar only: the list stops at them); `home` `end` go to the first and last row, `pgup` `pgdown` a page; `1`–`9` to the first to ninth project down the tree; `w` `W` to the next and previous row that needs you (a thread waiting on you, a coordinator with questions; in the slim strip a project with ⚑), round the ends, expanding a project that hides it and clearing a filter that does. `/` starts a filter: what is typed (letters that are keys elsewhere too) narrows the tree to the projects, threads (by task id, title or thread id) and coordinators that match, with the project of each match kept over it and every inactive project's rows searched; a line under the header shows it (`/doc▏` while typing); `backspace` takes a letter back, `enter` keeps the filter (the keys move again) and `esc` clears it. The cursor stays on its row while the row shows, else goes to the first. The filter is each console's own and stays across its screens. The cursor starts on the row you are on, is drawn in reverse video in the accent colour instead of it, and is the view's (`view.sidesel`, `SideSel`); which area has the keyboard is each console's own. While the sidebar has it in a session no key, paste or prefix-twice reaches the pane; the prefix commands still work, and the status bar says `preview · ⏎ to type here` (`preview…` while the cursor hasn't rested yet). **The footer** (`sideFooter`), below the tree in the full sidebar: a faint rule; two lines on the highlighted row (the cursor's, else the one you are on): its full name and state (`demo · active · paused`, `demo coordinator · working`, `T232 Sidebar rework (t-0216)`), then what goes with it (`3 threads · coordinator working`; `context 38% · remote control on · 2 questions waiting`; `working · 40% · PR #290 · CI fails`); then the key bar: the keys that do something on that row (`⏎ open · ␣ deactivate · ? help` on a project, `⏎ attach · ← project · ? help` on a session's row, `⏎ start` on a coordinator not running, `→ unfold` on a folded project), or the way to give the sidebar the keyboard (`prefix+tab sidebar · prefix+? help`, `tab sidebar · ? help` on the dashboard) while it doesn't have it; `? help` gives way first when they don't fit. The update hint (**Version**, above) takes a line below it. The sidebar's keys show only there: the dashboard's footer says `esc back to the list · tab next area` meanwhile. A click on a pane gives the keyboard back. A test (`TestEverySidebarClickHasKey`) keeps every sidebar click and menu item reachable from the keys.
  - **A click gives its area the keyboard**, as `tab` does, with its border in the accent colour: a list row the list, the details panel the panel, the info panel the info panel, a sidebar row the sidebar, with its cursor on the clicked row, so the arrows move it at once. A click on a sidebar row that attaches a session (a coordinator, a thread) keeps the keyboard in the sidebar, in the session too, so the arrows go on moving through the tree; a click on the pane, `esc` or the prefix then `tab` gives it to the session. `enter` on such a row gives the session the keyboard instead, as before. Which area has the keyboard carries between the dashboard and the session: the sidebar keeps it across the prefix then `d`, a popup over the session and back. Popups and menus take the keys while they are open, whatever has the keyboard.
  - Its state (width, slim, the keyboard's row) is part of the view (`view.Sidebar`, `view.SideSel`, §3.3). The icon set is each console's own (above).
- **Footer.** It starts with `≡ menu`, then lists only the keys that apply to the selected row: `enter attach` (a coordinator's or a thread's session), `enter show` (a task in NEEDS YOU, or a paired server's need in the all-projects view) or `enter open`. `?` lists every key. Each hint is a button: a click presses its key (a popup's action row too: `esc close`, `enter show`, `y yes`, `n no`).
- **Header, footer and rows by the layout** (§5.3, `[header]`, `[footer]`, `[rows]`; each console's own). A hidden header gives its row to the list; a compact footer is one row, the message while there is one (the prefix's keys win while the prefix waits), else the key hints, whose buttons still take clicks, and no rule; a hidden footer has no rows, and `?` and the `≡` menu (a right-click) still list the keys. What a hidden part carries still shows, drawn over the list without moving it, for as long as it lasts: the server's alarms (`▲ server down`, `▲ no agent installed`, `◌ loading…`) and the `⚑ N` badge of a hidden sidebar over the list's first row at the right (the badge takes its click there), a message (an error, `working…`) over its last row at the left. Compact rows (`density = "compact"`) take out every extra line and never a row's state, paused, inactive or needs-you: no details under the selected thread where the panel doesn't show (`enter` and the details popup show them), the project's task counts on its section header's line before the state label (which gives way first when narrow), an unselected mod card as its header line alone (selected, its lines show), and the sidebar's footer as its key bar alone (a glyph's tooltip shows there). Attached, a compact status bar (`[status_bar] style`) has no rule above it and the pane gets the row; it is the view's, so every console of the view follows.
- **Mouse.** Every key has a mouse path, and a test (`TestEveryKeyHasMousePath`) keeps it so: each action of the keys table is in the `≡` menu, whose items press the very key, or names its own mouse path (the arrows: the wheel or a click).
  - A click selects a row and a double-click opens it, as `enter` does (a coordinator or a thread attaches, a task in NEEDS YOU shows); the wheel moves the selection. Over the details panel the wheel scrolls it; another row's details start at the top. The divider and the sidebar's border can be dragged; a click on the sidebar's tree shows a project's dashboard, attaches a coordinator or a thread, and a click on its `PROJECTS` header shows every project's NEEDS YOU (**All projects**, above), the sidebar keeping the keyboard on the header. A click gives its area the keyboard (**From the keyboard**, above).
  - A right-click on a row opens a small menu of its actions: open or attach it, the project's popup, its Tasks tab and its Inbox tab. On the sidebar's tree the same for a project (show its dashboard, open its coordinator, its popup, tasks and inbox, fold or unfold it, activate or deactivate it, pin or unpin it; for an inactive one, expand or collapse it instead of opening its coordinator and folding; on the divider, fold or unfold it; on an expanded inactive project's coordinator row, `start the coordinator`, which activates the project), its coordinator or a thread. A right-click elsewhere in the list, and the footer's `≡ menu`, open the menu of every action. Menus take the arrows, `enter` and `esc` too, and show them in their action row; each item's key is right-aligned beside it, in the accent colour.
  - Popups (the project popup, settings, help, a question) take clicks: the project popup's tabs, a row to select it (a double-click opens a task), a setting's own line to select it and, once selected, a second click to change it as `enter` does (K3; yolo mode still asks first), the `−` `+` after a selected number's value (parallel threads, auto-close days) as `-` and `+` do, and the action row's hints, which press their keys. A click outside the popup closes it as `esc` does (popups have no close button); the wheel moves through its list or scrolls it.
  - Popups and menus are each console's own; what they do acts on the view, as the keys do, so the other consoles follow. Holding Shift selects text as usual in most terminals.
- **The key table** (small and fixed in v0.1: shortcuts speak one language). A prefix command means the same everywhere: in a session, on the dashboard, over any popup (also over a session) and with the sidebar holding the keyboard. It never reaches a popup as a plain key: the prefix takes the next key itself, and a command that opens or goes somewhere closes the popups first. A plain key that is also a prefix command means the same as it (on the dashboard prefix then a dashboard key runs that key), or the screen doesn't take it. `TestNoPlainKeyIsAPrefixCommand` (`internal/tui/keyclash_test.go`) fails when a plain key on any screen is a prefix command with another meaning: it presses every prefix command's key on the list, every popup (the project popup on each tab, the task list and a shown task in each status), the menu and the sidebar. Questions (`y` yes, `n` no, `esc` cancel; no other key answers) and prompts (typed text) are left out; the prefix answers them no.
- **Action ids.** Every action of the list, of the sidebar and of the info and details panels has a stable id that names one behaviour (`project.next`, `details.toggle`, `side.pin`, `panel.fold`; `internal/tui/actions.go`, `sideActions`, `panelActions`, **Mod sections** above), and so do the popups' and the prefix's actions the action binding will name (`task.delegate`, `library.delete`; `coreActions` in `internal/tui/keybind.go`). Ids are API: a keymap binds them (`id = ["key", …]`) and a rename keeps the old id as an alias. The remap layer (`ApplyKeymap`, fed by the enabled keymap mods, §5.3; there is no `[keymap]` table in `config.toml`) turns a typed key into its action's default key where keys come in (the list, the sidebar, the prefix on the dashboard and in a session), so the help, the footers, the menus and the hints show the bound keys and nothing behind them changes. It refuses the escape hatches (`esc`, `ctrl+c`, the prefix, and `d` `q` `r`, which are only prefix commands: `prefix+d`, `prefix+q`, `prefix+r`), the fixed actions (`tab`, the sidebar's `esc` and `1-9`, quit), two actions on one key, and a prefix command's key that means something else in a popup or the sidebar, so the key table above still holds.

  **Prefix commands** (the prefix: Ctrl+B by default; hints write `prefix+<key>`):

  | Key | In a session | On the dashboard and over a popup |
  |---|---|---|
  | `d` | back to the dashboard, on every console of the view; the session keeps running | back to the bare list: the popups close (over a session: the dashboard, as in it) |
  | `q` | quit this console, as closing its window does: the view stays as it is, and the server, the sessions and other consoles keep running. No confirm | the same, from anywhere: the list, any popup, a prompt, a question, a menu, the sidebar or the details panel holding the keyboard |
  | `a` `i` `t` `,` `?` | open that popup over the session (below): the project popup (on the session's project; `t` on its Tasks tab, `i` on its Inbox tab), settings, help | the same popup, as the plain key: any popup open closes first (`a` on the Tasks tab opens the project popup anew, accepting nothing) |
  | `]` `[` | back to the dashboard, which runs that key: next / previous project's coordinator | the same, as the plain key; over a session, back to the dashboard first |
  | `{` `}` | narrow / widen the projects sidebar; the pane follows | the same, as the plain key; the popup stays |
  | `\|` | show or hide the info panel beside a thread's or a coordinator's pane (**Info panel**, below); the pane follows | the panel on the right: the details panel, as the plain `\|` (over a session: back to it, which toggles its info panel) |
  | `b` | the sidebar: the slim strip, hidden, full again | the same, as the plain key; the popup stays |
  | `tab` | the keyboard to the next area: the projects sidebar and back to the pane, which gets no keys meanwhile | the same, as the plain `tab`: the list, the details panel, the sidebar; the popups close (over a session: back to it, the sidebar holding the keyboard) |
  | `;` | back to the last place: the area, sidebar row and session the keyboard was in before (the thread you were typing in, say), attaching that session or going back to the dashboard; again goes back where it came from. Each console's own | the same, as the plain `;`; over a session, back to it, which does it |
  | `r` | turn the focused coordinator's remote control on or off, after a `y` in the status bar (§11.2); the status bar then says what happened and that it lasts until the coordinator is started anew | the same for the selected project's coordinator, after a `y` in a question (`no coordinator runs for <slug>` when none does); over a session, back to it, which asks |
  | `R` | on a paired server's pane (§16.12): ask that server for a fresh repaint (`repainting from mini`); on this server's own panes it says it only repaints a paired server's; in the session's `≡` menu as `repaint from its server` on a remote pane. Action id `pane.repaint`, rebindable | over a session, back to it, which asks; else `prefix+R repaints a paired server's pane: open one first` |
  | the prefix | send the prefix itself to the program | nothing (no program to send it to) |
  | `esc` | cancel | cancel |
  | anything else | cancel | `prefix+<key> isn't a command; ? lists them` |

  Only `d`, `q`, the sidebar commands, `|`, `;` (within the session: there is no dashboard to go back to), `r`, `R` and the prefix work in `tm attach` and `tm project open`, which have no dashboard to go back to. After the prefix the footer (or the status bar) lists the commands, every one of them (`TestEveryPrefixCommandListed`).

  **Plain keys on the dashboard** (the list; with the sidebar or the details panel holding the keyboard, their own keys first, then these):

  | Key | Action |
  |---|---|
  | `↑` `↓` (`k` `j`), `pgup` `pgdown` | move the selection, by a row or by ten |
  | `enter` | attach to the selected session; a task in NEEDS YOU opens the project popup's Tasks tab on it; a paired server's need in the all-projects NEEDS YOU shows that project's remote dashboard |
  | `tab` / `shift+tab` | the keyboard to the next / previous area: the list, the details panel, the projects sidebar (its keys above); as `prefix+tab` |
  | `a` `i` `t` `,` `?` | the project popup (overview, inbox, tasks, settings, keys; `t` opens it on the Tasks tab, `i` on the Inbox tab (every unhandled item, read-only: the coordinator handles them): tasks grouped NEEDS YOU / IN MOTION / ON DECK / BACKLOG / DONE (collapsed, `b`), done tasks last until archived so `x` can send one back; `enter` shows one, **A task, shown**, above), the settings for every project, the help; as their prefix commands |
  | `]` / `[` | open the next / previous project's coordinator; as the prefix commands (while the details panel has the keyboard and shows mods' sections, they select its sections' headers instead: **Mod sections**) |
  | `{` / `}`, `b` | narrow / widen the projects sidebar; the slim strip, hidden, full again; as the prefix commands |
  | `space` | activate the project shown, or deactivate it (§5.1); the project's section header ends in its state label (`● active — space to deactivate`, `⊘ inactive — space to activate`; a click on it does what `space` does, and `∥ paused` follows the name while paused) and the footer says what `space` does (`space deactivate`, `space activate`); asks first while its coordinator or threads run, as in the sidebar; in the ≡ menu as `activate / deactivate the project` |
  | `n` | new project: a prompt for its name, which becomes the slug (`Demo App` → `demo-app`); as `prefix+n`, which opens the prompt as a popup over a session (like `prefix+a`) or over the dashboard, never by way of the dashboard; the project is created and you stay where you were |
  | `<` / `>` | narrow / widen the list beside the details panel |
  | `;` | back to the last place; as `prefix+;` |
  | `\|` | show or hide the details panel; as `prefix+\|` |

  There is no plain `q` (`prefix+q` quits) and no refresh key: the dashboard polls every second, and an open task list reloads every 5 seconds (`prefix+r` is remote control).

  **Plain keys in popups.** `esc` closes the topmost popup or menu, and no other key does. Lists take `↑` `↓` (`k` `j`), `pgup` `pgdown`, `enter`: every list, the dashboard's, its details panel and the menus too. Each key means one thing, with no undocumented aliases. None of a popup's keys is a prefix command:

  | Popup | Its keys |
  |---|---|
  | help | `↑` `↓` scroll |
  | project popup | `←` `→` or `1`–`7` switch tabs; Overview: `+` adds a repository, `x` removes one (asks first); Settings: `enter` / `space` change, `+` `-` step a number; Tasks: `enter` shows the task, `D` delegate, `A` accept, `x` send back, `c` the coordinator; Library: `enter` reads the file, `d` deletes it, `D` deletes its thread's files (each asks first) |
  | a shown task | `D` `A` `x` `c` as on the Tasks tab; `esc` goes back to it |
  | inbox, settings | the list's keys; the settings' `enter` / `space` / `+` `-` change one, and `x` on a project's own setting makes it follow All projects again |
  | menus (`≡`, a right-click) | `enter` / `space` pick |
  | question / prompt | `y` yes, `n` no, `esc` cancel, and no other key answers (`enter` included; K1) / typed text, `enter` ok, `ctrl+u` clear |
  | the prefix key (settings) | the next `ctrl+<key>` is the new prefix |

- **Attaching.** `enter` shows the selected session in the view (`view.attach`), on every console of the view. Attaching gives the whole screen to the pane, rendered from the client's mirror emulator (§3.3), with a one-line status bar at the bottom that the client draws, and a faint rule between the pane and it, so a program's own footer (Claude Code's model and mode line) doesn't run into it. The projects sidebar stays on the left; the status bar runs across the whole window, under the sidebar too (which ends above the rule, its footer with it), and the rule under the sidebar and the pane (the info panel reaches down to the bar). It has no fill and is muted. Left, it says where you are: the session's state glyph in its colour, `project › pane` (`terminatr › T232 Sidebar rework`, `terminatr › coordinator`; a plain session, outside a project, `s-3 › zsh`), then its progress (`40% ▰▰▱▱▱ 1/3 ▸ Key clash check`; a coordinator without todos its context use, `context 38%`), why it is blocked, queued prompts, `remote control on` while a coordinator's is and `model <name> on next start` while a coordinator's agent can't switch to the model its setting wants (§8.2 **Models**), then a note: `preview · ⏎ to type here` while the sidebar's cursor previews, the info panel's keys while it has the keyboard, or a message in passing. When it doesn't fit, the step and the rest give way first, then the bar, then the note shrinks to `preview`, then the title is cut; the percent stays. Right, the global keys in the accent colour: `prefix+? help · prefix+d dashboard · ≡ menu`. After the prefix it lists the commands instead, keys in the accent colour, and `prefix again sends it`. There is no breadcrumb line above the pane: the bar is it. It never asks a question: prefix then `r` asks in a dialog over the session. There are no window buttons and no split panes: one session shows at a time. Each is a button for the mouse (below). The client polls `session.list` for it, and the project folders for the sidebar. Sessions started from the dashboard get the window's size less the sidebar and those two rows, so nothing is cropped. (`tm attach` and `tm project open` show the pane beside the sidebar in a view of their own; `tm attach` has the status bar and the rule above it on every session's pane, a plain session's too.)
- **Popups over a session.** The prefix then `a`, `i`, `t`, `,` or `?`, and the project popup, its Tasks tab and its Inbox tab in a sidebar row's menu, open the dashboard's popup over the session rather than going back to the dashboard. It is this console's own: the view stays on the session, on every console, and the session keeps running. The popup draws over the session's screen, dimmed, under a header naming the session (`tm s-4 · demo coordinator`), with the popup's keys in its own frame; it works as on the dashboard. The screen under it stays live: output keeps flowing there while the popup is open, from a stream of the session that sends nothing (no keys, no size), and a synchronized update (mode 2026) shows once it is complete. Closing it (`esc`, or a click outside it) attaches the same session again. A key that can't open its popup (`a` with no project to show) goes straight back to the session, and the status bar says why until the next key. The prefix then `d` from the popup goes to the dashboard, as in the session; an action that shows another session (opening a coordinator) attaches that one; and should the view go back to its dashboard meanwhile (another console's prefix then `d`), the popup stays open over the dashboard instead.
- **Thread panes.** A pane whose session is a thread's is like any other, in the dashboard's attach and in `tm attach` (which then shows the status bar too): keys, paste, the mouse and focus reports reach it, and typing claims its size (§3.3). The user talks to coordinators, though, so the first input a console sends a thread's pane during an attach (a key, a paste or a click that reaches the program) adds a `takeover` inbox item for the thread's coordinator and a journal line (`human thread.takeover t-0004`), so the coordinator learns that the user intervened. Nothing asks and nothing shows; it happens once per thread per attach of that console, not per key.
- **Info panel**. Right of a thread's pane, in the dashboard's attach and in `tm attach` alike, a read-only panel shows where the thread stands, live from the same poll as the sidebar (the project folder and the ticker's state): its task's id, title (bold, in the accent colour) and status, a **STEPS** section with the progress bar, once, and the steps with `✓` on those done and the first open one marked as under way; the thread's id and state (`blocked` with its reason), its progress, what it does now and what it waits on (`needs you`, from `tm status --needs-you`); while a question menu is open (§3.3, **Ask**), its state says `blocked question open` and a **QUESTION OPEN** section lists each question with its options by number, as `tm thread show` does; its PR in the ticker's words (`#70 open, checks pending`, `1 check failed`, `conflicts`), with `behind main` when GitHub says the branch is behind its base; a **LAST REPORT** section with the report's first lines and its age (never its `## Next` items, which are the coordinator's); the names of the files its reports attached (`attached  chart.png, plan.md`; names only, no paths); the branch, the worktree and when the thread was last active; then the mods' `details.section`s for the thread, from the same trees as the dashboard's details panel shows on its row, below those lines unless the layout's `[details] sections` puts them above (**Mod sections**). A plain session has none.
  - **Beside a coordinator** (`internal/tui/coordpanel.go`) the same panel shows its project as the /tm mod pane does (§8.6, **Mods**), from the same state (`server.ProjectWatchOf`, what `project.watch` sends, §3.3), read with the same poll: the project and `3 need you · 2 in inbox · 5 threads`; the coordinator's context use (`context 84k / 200k · 42%`, faint, yellow from `[ui] context_hint` with `c or click: save and clear (it saves what it learned first)`, red from 80%; `c` while the panel has the keyboard (`panel.save-clear`), or a click on that line, saves and clears the coordinator (`tm clear`, §7.5 **Auto-clear**), the status bar saying what came of it, and a paired server's project shows `past the context hint: save and clear it there` instead; as the sidebar's row; none until the coordinator's mod reported a turn); **NEEDS YOU** (held prompt queues with `tm agent explain <session>`, tasks in review, threads' questions, failed checks, blocked tasks, each `T63 ◆ in review Dashboard pane`, in the words `prompts held`, `in review`, `asks you`, `checks failed`, `blocked`, with the question or the PR under it and `asked the coordinator: accept` once asked; a long one wraps two cells in), or `Nothing waits for you.`; the coordinator's open questions lead NEEDS YOU (§7.5 **Questions**): `▲ 2 questions waiting · a or click answers`, and a click on it, or `a` while the panel has the keyboard (the status bar's hint then names it), has the coordinator open them (`questions.open`, the status bar saying what came of it): the status entry's fallback for a Codex coordinator, an older Claude, or mods or the band off; **INBOX**, a row per kind per subject, the kind in words (`PR opened`, `checks failed`), with its repeats as `×N`; **THREADS**, the thread, its task, its state and steps (`t-0060 T64 ▲ asks you 2/4`), what it does now (`▸ Write the tests`) or its task's title, and its PR, faint when stopped or done; **ON DECK**, the first five tasks with their state's glyph, and `and N more`; then the mods' `coordinator.section`s, at the bottom unless the layout places them among those blocks (**Mod sections**). The built-in `ticker` mod's (§5.3 **Built-in mods**) is the **TICKER** section there, one faint row per timer with the labels in a column (`PRs  40s ago · next 1m20s`, or `3m ago · due`; `synced  1m ago`; `PR host  ok`, in red `failing` while PR polls fail), a timer not known yet (no PR check yet) having no row. Whatever that mod does (on, off, failed, or drawing in the sidebar instead), core warns of a failing PR host in the panel's head: while the ticker's PR polls fail for a project with a PR host, a red `▲ PR host failing · inbox` line under the counts. The /tm pane keeps its own `Ticker` rows. Its sections are headed as the dashboard's (UPPERCASE, a faint rule). It has no buttons, since the user asks the coordinator in their own words: a click on a task (a need, an inbox row, a task on deck) opens the task view on it, on a running thread's rows shows that thread's pane, on a PR opens it in the browser. Width, `prefix+|`, dragging, the keyboard and `ui.json` are the thread panel's; `enter` there says to click a task. It replaces the /tm pane in tm, which doesn't open by itself (`[agent_mod] pane`, below).
  - **Beside a paired server's pane** (`internal/tui/remotepanel.go`; §16.12) the panel is drawn from this server's mirror of that server (`peer.list`, §16.10: the project's ProjectWatch and its trimmed sessions), never from a request to it, so nothing more crosses (§16.9). Its head names the server, on a line of its own (a long name is cut, never the words under it), then the link and how fresh the copy is: `◉ linked 38 ms · synced 2s ago`; down, `○ offline 4m · relay · last seen 14:02`, and everything under the head dimmed, its last values kept. Beside a coordinator, its project as the remote dashboard draws it: the project, `coordinator ● working · opus`, the counts, its context use, NEEDS YOU, THREADS (task, state, steps, PR), ON DECK, without the key and command hints. Beside a thread: its task's id, title and status; **STEPS** with the progress bar and the step under way (the step texts stay there); **THREAD** with its state, model, todos, what it does now, context use, `needs you`, `question  open in its pane` while one is, its PR in that ticker's words (`checked 30s ago`, on the peer's clock) and its checks (`passing`, `failing`, `running`), and how many reports. It has no mod sections (mods are per server). Its width, `prefix+|`, dragging and scrolling are the panel's; nothing in it acts on that server: no click opens a task or the questions, `enter` and `a` say `mini's: view-only here`, and the status bar's hint lists only `↑ ↓ scroll · esc back to the pane`. A click on a PR opens it in the browser, on a running thread's row (beside a coordinator) shows that thread's pane while the server shares its panes with this one. A project no longer shared, or a server no longer paired, says so.
  - It takes its columns from the pane, above the status bar, which runs under both; it is part of the view (`view.Info`, `view.info`, §3.3) and the session is sized to what is left. It is 40 columns wide by default (its border included), at least 24, and shows only while the pane keeps its 60 columns beside the sidebar: in a narrower window it shrinks, then hides, and comes back when the window is wide enough. `prefix+|` hides and shows it (it then stays hidden in every window until shown again; in a window too narrow the status bar says how many columns it needs); over a plain session it says that the panel shows beside a thread's or a coordinator's pane. Dragging its border resizes it; `ui.json` keeps the width and whether it is off, for new views, as for the sidebar.
  - A click on the task opens the project popup's Tasks tab on it over the session, the task shown (the dashboard's `t`; `tm attach`, which has no dashboard, says so); a click on the PR opens it in the browser (`open`, or `xdg-open` on Linux). A click anywhere else in it gives it the keyboard, as `prefix+tab` does after the sidebar (pane, sidebar, info panel, pane): `↑` `↓` (`k` `j`), `pgup` `pgdown`, `home` `end` scroll it, `enter` shows the task, `esc` or `tab` give the keyboard back to the pane; meanwhile no key or paste reaches the pane, and the status bar lists those keys. The wheel scrolls it; a click on the pane gives the pane the keyboard back.
- **The mouse in a session.** Inside the pane the program gets the mouse whenever it tracks it (Claude Code does); everything else is tm's. The status bar's buttons run their command: `prefix+? help` opens the help over the session, `prefix+d dashboard` goes back, and while a question shows (remote control), `y yes` says yes and any other click no; after the prefix each listed command is a button. `≡`, a right-click on the status bar, or a right-click on a pane whose program doesn't take the mouse (a shell) opens the session's menu, drawn over the pane: every prefix command, less those that don't apply (the dashboard's in `tm attach`, remote control off a coordinator). A click on the pane gives it the keyboard back from the sidebar. A right-click on a sidebar row opens its menu: a project's dashboard, coordinator, popup, tasks and inbox (the popups over the session); attach a thread. Shift-drag keeps native text selection (Option-drag in some macOS terminals).
- **Copying text** (`internal/tui/selection.go`). A drag over a pane whose program doesn't take the mouse selects text in tm: the cells are drawn in reverse video while dragging (the selection is the mirror's, so it stays on its text as the screen scrolls), and the release copies it, soft-wrapped rows joined and trailing blanks trimmed, with a `copied N lines` note in the status bar. A click or a key clears it. A program that takes the mouse (Claude Code) selects itself and copies with OSC 52. Both go to the outer terminal of the console the user works in as OSC 52 (`ESC ] 52 ; c ; <base64> BEL`, at most 1 MiB of text), so the copy lands on the clipboard of the machine the user sits at, over SSH too; a pane's OSC 52 is forwarded only from the focused pane, and only by the view's latest typist, so two consoles on one view don't both copy. OSC 52 reads are never answered. The outer terminal must allow OSC 52 writes (iTerm2: "Applications in terminal may access clipboard"; tmux: `set -g set-clipboard on`); Shift-drag stays the fallback.
- **One pane.** A session shows alone, beside the sidebar (there are no split panes, zoom or window buttons). When its session exits, the view goes back to the dashboard and says why. A session the server relaunches under the same id (its stream closes with `session restarting`, a remote control change, §11.2) is attached again, without a resize, and the status bar says `s-4 is back`. Which session shows is the view's (§3.3), so it shows on every console of the view and stays while the dashboard shows; `enter` on another session shows that one instead. On the dashboard the prefix then a key is that key, so the same keys do the same things in both places; the prefix then `q` quits from anywhere.
- **Project switching.** While attached, the prefix then `]` or `[` jumps to the next or previous project's coordinator. These run on the dashboard, so they are the dashboard's own keys and no key is taken from the pane but the prefix (the popups open over the session instead, above). "Next" is relative to the project last attached to; the status bar always names the current project.
- **Projects.** A project row with no running coordinator says so; `enter` on it starts the coordinator (as `tm project open` does) and attaches. Opening never activates: for an inactive project (§5.1) `enter` on its dashboard rows, `c` and `tm project open` start nothing and say `demo is inactive: activate it first (space on it in the sidebar, or tm project activate demo)`; a click on its row still shows its dashboard. The one way to start it by opening is its coordinator's row in the sidebar's tree, once expanded, which activates it (**Projects sidebar**, above). `]` and `[` skip inactive projects.
- **Rendering.** The dashboard uses Bubble Tea v2 and Lip Gloss v2. The attached panes bypass Bubble Tea: a cell renderer per pane draws dirty rows from its mirror (§3.3).
- **Alerts stay in the terminal.** Terminatr sends no desktop notifications (no `osascript`, Notification Center or `notify-send`), and the client does not pass a pane's OSC 9/777 notifications to the outer terminal. When a session becomes `blocked`, or a thread reports, every client (dashboard or attached) rings the bell on its terminal; the event itself shows in the projects sidebar's state glyphs, the status bar, NEEDS YOU (coordinators) and the coordinator's inbox and nudges. The client re-emits a pane's OSC 52 clipboard writes to the outer terminal while attached. OSC 52 reads are denied.

---

## 5. On-disk state

### 5.1 Layout

```
~/.terminatr/                         TERMINATR_HOME
  config.toml                          user settings: keys, icons, all-projects and per-project settings, agents among them (§11.2), and each project's repo list   [human; tm only from the TUI's settings popups and tm project repo]
  ui.json                              this console's layout: details panel on or off (details_place, details_rows), list width; the projects sidebar (its mode), the info panel, and the tree's folds with the mods' sections' (sections_folded), that new views start with (§4); layouts.<id>: its changes under each layout mod (§5.3)  [tm]
  agents/<name>.toml                   user agent manifests (§8.2)                   [human]
  mods/<id>/                           installed user mods, each with its mod.toml manifest (§5.3)  [human, through tm mod install or the Mods tab's i]
  .trash/<slug>-<UTC time>/            deleted projects' folders (tm project delete)  [tm, on the human's word]
  run/  tm.sock human server.lock server.pid                                    [server]
  run/s/<id>/                          per-session launch files: the agent's settings and plugin (hooks, terminatr's mod), §8.6  [server]
  run/bin/tm                           the running server's pinned binary (§3.6)     [server]
  state/sessions.json                  live sessions, for resume (§3.6)              [server]
  state/views.json                     the server-owned views but own ones: screen, the session shown, selection, current project, sidebar (§3.3)  [server]
  state/mods/<id>/                     a user mod's own data, and store.json, its $.store (§5.3)  [the mod, through the broker]
  state/peer/identity.key              the server's Ed25519 identity key, PKCS #8 PEM, 0600; its SHA-256 is the server id (§16.7)  [tm peer, made at the first pairing (§16.8)]
  state/peer/peers.json                the paired servers: alias, pinned public key, relay URL (§16.7)  [tm peer, the human's (§16.8)]
  state/peer/peers.lock                held while tm peer changes peers.json (§16.8)  [tm peer]
  cache/mods/<id>/                     a code mod's bundle, bundle.json and host.json (§3.7, Supervisor)  [server]
  state/repos-moved                    the mark that the projects' repo lists have moved from PROJECT.md to config.toml (below)  [server]
  state/ticker.json                    what the ticker already reported: thread states, PRs, nudges, syncs (§7.5)  [server]
  state/links.json                     the message counter, each link direction's rate bucket and each pair's breaker (§7.9); the outbox epoch and sequences (§16.11)  [tm]
  state/link/outbox/<alias>/<seq>.json a message or receipt waiting for the paired server <alias> (§16.11)  [tm]
  state/slots.json                     the coordinators a thread cap refused, waiting for a slot (§9)  [tm, ticker]
  logs/server.log                      rotated at 10 MB, 3 kept                      [server]
  logs/mods/<id>.log                   a user mod's log, rotated at 1 MiB, and the broker's audit lines about it (§3.7, Supervisor; §5.3)  [server, from the mod host and the broker]
  logs/service.log                     a launchd-started server's own output (macOS service, §3.1)  [launchd]
  worktrees/<slug>/<id>-<title-slug>/  thread worktrees: plain git checkouts, nothing terminatr-owned inside (§9)
  projects/<slug>/                     one project = the coordinator's cwd
    PROJECT.md                         TOML front matter (goal, optional code_host, azure_url) + standing instructions   [coordinator, human]
    AGENTS.md                          generated role file; CLAUDE.md -> AGENTS.md   [tm]
    CONTEXT.md                         living context: current plan, conventions, decisions   [coordinator]
    MEMORY.md, memory/*.md             durable lessons and decisions                 [coordinator]
    TASKS.md                           the task board (§6)                           [tm, on the coordinator's calls]
    tasks/ARCHIVE.md                   archived tasks                                [tm]
    JOURNAL.md                         append-only: one line per tm action + reason; the last archive_journal_days days  [tm]
    journal/<yyyy-mm>.md.gz            older journal lines, by month (gzip members appended; zcat reads them)  [tm, ticker]
    inbox/*.md, inbox/done/*.md        events for the coordinator (§7.5)             [tm]
    questions.json                     the coordinator's open questions to the user (§7.5, **Questions**)  [tm, on the coordinator's calls]
    messages/<other>/M-<n>.md          messages with linked project <other>, both ways: TOML front matter + the text (§7.9)  [tm]
    messages/@<alias>/<other>/M-<fp>-<n>.md   the same with project <other> on the paired server <alias> (§16.11)  [tm]
    messages/<other>/M-<n>/            a message's attached files                     [tm]
    inbox/done/<yyyy-mm>.tar.gz        handled items older than archive_inbox_days, by the month they were raised  [ticker]
    threads/archive/<id>.tar.gz        a resolved thread's folder, packed archive_threads_days after resolve  [ticker]
    threads/archive/index              one line per archived thread: id, resolved date, task, title  [ticker]
    threads/<id>/
      thread.toml                      id, title, task, agent, branch, worktree, session ids, state, adopted  [tm]
      brief.md                         the thread's scoped brief (§7.1)              [tm]
      task.md                          task text plus every forwarded prompt         [tm]
      REPORT.md                        the thread's latest report, stored by `tm report` (§7.2)  [tm]
      reports/<n>.md                   earlier reports, kept for the record          [tm]
      STATUS.md                        latest progress, stored by `tm status` (§7.3) [tm]
      library/                         files the thread attached for the user        [tm]
    uploads/                           files the user gave the project               [human]
```

- **Writer discipline.** Every write is a write to a temp file followed by `rename`, under a per-file lock (a hidden `.<file>.lock`, `flock`). Files marked `[tm]` are only ever rewritten by `tm`. A human hand-editing `TASKS.md` is tolerated: `tm` re-parses the file, and if it can't, it refuses to write and reports the line number.
- **`config.toml` is the human's.** Besides the projects' tables and the all-projects `[defaults]` table (§11.2) it holds `[keys] prefix`, `[ui] icons`, `[ui] theme` and `[ui] layout` (§5.3: an enabled mod's id, the built-in one when unset), `[ui] context_hint` (§4, §8.6 **Context reminder**; 40 when unset), `[ui] needs_first` (§4; off when unset) and `[ui] update_check` (§10.1; on unless `false`), `[agent_mod] enabled`, `[agent_mod] band` and `[agent_mod] pane` (§8.6, **Mods**; `enabled` and `pane` off, `band` on when unset), the table that was `[mods]` until user mods took the word (its `enabled`, `band` and `pane` are still read for each key `[agent_mod]` doesn't set; any other key directly under `[mods]` is unknown), the user mods' `[mods.<id>]` tables (§5.3; a `[mods.<id>.settings]` table alone implies its mod's table; an unknown key in one is reported by `tm doctor`, a key of the wrong type only stops that mod), each project's repo list (`repos` in its `[projects.<slug>]` table, below), and each agent's model settings under `[agents.<name>]` (§8.2 **Models**), laid over what the installed agent lists: `hide = ["…"]` leaves listed models out, `add = ["…"]` offers models the agent takes but doesn't list, and `default_model = "…"` names the model a launch passes when none is chosen (unset or `""`: none, the agent runs its own default; one the agent doesn't offer now passes none, and `tm doctor` warns). The older `models = [{ name, about }, …]` list (a full catalog, from before tm asked the agents) is still read: its names count as added, its about lines are not used, `tm doctor` warns, and the next save from the Models page writes the new keys in its place. All are read in one place (`config.Load`); a name not one word, or one listed twice, fails it as a bad safety setting does, an unknown key under `[agents.<name>]` is ignored with a `tm doctor` warning. The Settings popup's Models page writes an agent's keys in one edit; `[[agents.<name>.models]]` tables are read but left to the user's hand. The older top-level `default_agent` (from older versions) is still read as the all-projects `coordinator_agent` when `[defaults]` doesn't set it; saving All projects' Coordinator agent removes the line. An unknown key in a project's table or `[defaults]` is an error, so a typo can't leave a safety setting at its default; one under `[keys]`, `[ui]` or `[mods]` is ignored and `tm doctor` warns about it. People edit it by hand, and `tm` writes it only when the human changes a setting in the TUI's settings popups (§4, §11.2), or activates, pauses, archives or deletes a project (`tm project activate|deactivate|pause|resume|archive|unarchive|delete`, human only), or changes a project's repos (`tm project repo`, the project popup's overview; the coordinator only removes one, its `tm` command running in the server, §11.1). That write edits the one line (or appends the table and the line), so comments, order and formatting stay as they were; it keeps the file's mode, parses the result and checks it says what was meant before the atomic rename, under the file's lock. A setting written in another form (a dotted key, an inline table) is left alone, and the popup says it can't change it there; a file that doesn't parse is never written over.
- **A project's lifecycle** is the human's (§10, §11.1). `active`, `paused` and `archived` are settings in its `config.toml` table (§11.2). Every project is **active** unless the user deactivated it (`active = false`; new and existing projects are active). Only an active project's coordinator and threads run: a server start or restart resumes them (§3.6), and an inactive project's stay **dormant**, neither started nor resumed, kept in `sessions.json` to resume when the user activates it. Activation is explicit only: `space` on its row in the sidebar or on its dashboard, the `Active` row in its popup, `tm project activate <slug>`, or starting its coordinator from the coordinator's row of the expanded project in the sidebar (§4), which activates it as part of starting it; opening, clicking, attaching or expanding it in the sidebar never activates. Activating resumes its dormant sessions at once. Deactivating (`space`, the `Active` row, `tm project deactivate <slug>`) stops its running coordinator and threads at once, after a confirmation when any runs (`y` in the TUI; on a terminal `y` to `Deactivate demo? This stops its coordinator, T3 and T5 now; they resume when you activate it. [y/N]`, otherwise `--yes`), keeping them dormant. While inactive, the project shows collapsed in the sidebar (§4); `tm project open`, `session.start` of its coordinator or a thread, and `tm thread start` / `restart` / `adopt` are refused (`project-inactive`, naming the way to activate it); and the ticker does no work for it, as for an archived project (§7.5), catching up on its first sweep after activation. The three states differ in what runs: **paused** parks the ticker's prompts while the agents keep running; **inactive** stops the agents too, but the project stays in the sidebar, collapsed; **archived** hides it (and is refused while agents run). They combine: an archived project's dormant sessions resume only once it is unarchived and active, and pausing an inactive project changes nothing until it is activated. A rename moves a project's dormant records with it (their project, cwd and brief). An **archived** project is hidden from the sidebar and the dashboard, and the ticker does nothing for it (what it knew is kept for an unarchive); archiving is refused (`sessions-running`) while its coordinator or a thread runs. `tm project list` still lists it, marked `(archived)`, and `tm project unarchive <slug>` brings it back. **Delete** moves the project folder to `~/.terminatr/.trash/<slug>-<UTC time>/` (a journal line goes with it), after a confirmation (the slug typed on a terminal, `--yes`, or `y` in the popup), and is refused while its agents run. Its threads' worktrees (which `tm doctor` then lists as leftovers) and branches stay where they are, and its `active`, `paused`, `archived`, `pinned`, `share` and `repos` lines are removed, so a new project of the same slug starts without them and approves no repository it didn't name (the journal line names the repos it had); its dormant sessions are dropped at the next activation or server start, their project being gone. Nothing in the trash is deleted by `tm`.
- **The slug is a project's only name** (PROJECT.md carries no display name). It is the name of its folder, lower case: letters `a-z`, digits and `-`, starting with a letter or digit, at most 64 characters. `tm project new`, `tm project rename` and the dashboard's `n` turn what is typed into a slug: lower case, each run of other characters one `-`, so `Demo App` makes `demo-app`, and say the slug used (`created project demo-app …`); text with no letter or digit is refused (`invalid-project`), as is a slug a project has already (`project-exists`). Commands that look a project up by slug still take only the exact slug. Every display says the slug: the sidebar, the dashboard, the popup's title and overview, `tm project list`, `tm context` (`Project: <slug>`), `AGENTS.md` and a thread's brief. A `name` key an older tm wrote into PROJECT.md is ignored, and dropped the next time tm rewrites the front matter (`tm project repo`); the project loads unchanged.
- **Repos**. A project's repo list, the repositories its threads may get worktrees of, is `repos = ["/abs/path", …]` in its `config.toml` table, the human's file, not `PROJECT.md`, which the coordinator writes: a Codex coordinator's sandbox has the project folder as its workspace and `config.toml` read-only, and a Claude coordinator keeps its `Edit` deny rule on it (§8.6; its Bash, unsandboxed, could still write the file, as it could the safety settings). It is not a setting: no settings popup shows it, `[defaults]` can't set it (an error, as for `active`), and `tm` writes it only from `tm project new --repo`, `tm project repo` and the project popup's overview, one line by the same line editor under the file's lock, an emptied list as `repos = []`. Only the user adds a repository (`tm project repo add` refuses an agent, `human-only`); the coordinator may remove one, its `tm` running in the server (§11.1), and an agent's `tm thread start --repo` names only a listed one. Archive and unarchive leave the list; rename carries it with the table; delete removes it. **The move:** an older `tm` kept the list in `PROJECT.md`'s front matter. A server's first start with this version (`project.MoveRepos`, before it resumes any agent) copies each project's list into its table, unless the table has one, journals it (`ticker project.repo.move <slug> to config.toml: <paths>`), drops the key from `PROJECT.md`, and then writes `state/repos-moved`.
